August 4, 2024 09:00 PM GMT

Asia Quantitative Strategy - Alpha Briefing | Asia Pacific

# Market Washout - Move Defensive and Wait for Soft Landing Green Light

A global growth scare and hawkish policy surprise saw a sharp unwind of strongly performing YTD trades (Japan and Taiwan equities, Asia Semis/Equipment, USDJPY). We see potential for further downside in the near term and move more defensive, reiterating our call to switch from Semis to Staples.

Consensus trade washout - stay defensive for now: We remain cautious on 3 drivers of the market volatility on 1-2 August. 1) We expect further rotation out of Semis for the reasons we laid out on 21 July. 2) Global growth concerns may remain elevated over the coming weeks given a paucity of tier 1 data and G7+China policy meetings. 3) Our macro strategists expect further USD/JPY carry trade unwinds. We recommend shifting defensive and highlight the low beta and down-market outperformance of India and ASEAN. We also screen for high quality, low beta and low momentum equities.

But look for opportunities: We do not recommend bottom-fishing in Asia semis given still high positioning, valuations, and relative performance. Instead, we think the indiscriminate weakness across Japan equities is presenting opportunities that should prove compelling if our US economics team's house call of a soft landing plays out. We retain exposure to Japan's nominal GDP reflation and corporate reform themes, with our new "Reaction to Earnings" dataset showing a skew toward strengthening investment theses and upward N12M EPS revisions.

Japan Equity Strategy: Our Macro Strategy team expect a further unwind of USD/ JPY carry trades, with their estimated fair value moving down to ¥140. We highlight FX sensitivity by sector and stocks in Exhibit 22 . In our Japan Focus List, we now remove Renesas Electronics (6723.T), UT Group (2146.T), and Isetan Mitsukoshi Holdings (3099.T), following our earlier removal of Tokyo Electron and Tokyo Semitsu on 21 July. In their place, we add NTT (9432.T), Kansai Electric Power (9503.T), and Fast Retailing (9983.T), increasing our defensive/domestic bias.

Flows/Positioning: EM recorded inflows driven by China, Taiwan, and South Korea. China inflows were largely contributed by domestic China funds, while offshore China funds and active long-only US and EU funds sold further. Underweights on India in EM fund allocations stand at 81bps given the sharply rising index weight. China now stands at 67bps underweight vs. benchmark for EM funds, while Global funds are 52bps underweight on Japan equities as of end-June. Japan saw foreign sell-off in the past week (ended July 26, 2024), with foreign investors net selling US \$9.9bn s. US\$5.0bn inflow from retail investors.

## Top of the Agenda

## Four drivers of a consensus trade washout

Last week saw a confluence of factors buffeting Asia/EM equity markets, all of which formed a quadruple whammy for Japan. We think the four most important drivers are:

• Reversal of semiconductor market leadership

• Global growth scare and geopolitical risks

• USD/JPY carry trade unwinds

• Equity momentum drawdown (particularly in Japan)

We have been concerned about all these aspects of the market and on 21 July we called for rotation out of Semis (Asia EM Equity Strategy: Shifting Risk-Reward – Take Profits on IT, Upgrade Consumer Staples to OW, July 21, 2024). Weakening global growth signals were one of the catalysts, alongside high valuations, crowded positioning, hyperbolic price patterns, and a historical pattern of defensive market rotation around the timing of first Fed cuts.

Exhibit 2: Asia / EM IT performance relative to EM vs. Average of US Services and Manufacturing PMI; YoY Growth
![](images/398dc45f4b69a4be7ecf18ff64aa8e1cf434f74d2e8f84390a9e5a9136b78d41.jpg)
Source: Factset, Datastream, Morgan Stanley Research. Note: Data as of July end, 2024.

In our last Alpha Briefing, we highlighted the narrow equity market breadth and risk of an momentum reversal given impending BoJ and FOMC meetings and our Macro Strategists' bullish JPY views (Asia Quantitative Strategy - Alpha Briefing: Correction: Weaker Risk-Reward on Semis; Pivot to Low Beta and High Shareholder Returns, 24 Jul 2024). In that note, we also trimmed our OW recommendation on Japan equities to +150bps, bringing it back in line with our bullish stance on Indian equities (see Exhibit 66 for our latest allocation).

## Where do we stand on these debates?

Last week was another reminder of the fat tail nature of markets, particularly as they adjust to a new risk/reward outlook without valuation or fund flow support. Here we outline our views on the key market debates:

## 1) Semiconductor leadership - Cautious

We do not recommend buying into weakness. Asia Semis relative performance since January 2023 peaked at +72% on 11 July but still stands at +49%. Our long-only positioning data as of end-June showed a record OW on Semis/Hardware (see Exhibit 36 ). We would expect further profit taking given mixed results for non-AI semis and an overall revenue peak on the horizon for 1Q25 on the latest ICTS outlook.

Exhibit 3: Asia-Pacific Semiconductors & Equipment P/Sales - Very elevated to own long-term history at 6.1x; inverse valuation relationship with subsequent 2-year returns evident
![](images/d1af944ddc6bbd596499d575c6377851f4898a74413852491faa5cb82ae7f0cb.jpg)
Source: MSCI, FactSet, Morgan Stanley Research. Data as of August 2, 2024

Exhibit 4: MSCI EM IT Performance vs. Global Economic Surprise Index - The latter has historically been a leading indicator, and now there is a disconnect between macro strength and stock performance driven by AI diffusion
![](images/08ac3471b8cac38b66ca20c594c56b3323c9fe519d0f9b780dabff4422879dcd.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research Note: Data as of 2 August, 2024

## 2) Global growth risks - Cautious

With broad-based slowing in the US labour market and weaker PMIs, we think the market will stay cautious over the coming weeks. We would tilt toward defensives over cyclicals until we see stronger signal of a soft landing (e.g., pickup in ISM Services and the reaction of consumer and business sentiment following a likely Fed cut in September). Ongoing debt-demographic-deflation challenges in China will likely keep the macro environment in Asia and EM subdued, outside of India (where we see a broad-based, secular domestic growth story). That said, our economists' house view is for a soft landing in the US, which will be important for finding support for key US-exposed markets such as Japan.

## 3) USD/JPY - Bearish

We have seen the largest two-week change in levered funds' net JPY positions, but positioning is not yet clean. Our Macro Strategy team sees further JPY carry trade unwinds, with their USD/JPY fair value falling to ¥140 (JPY | Carry the Pain Inside 2 August 2024).

Exhibit 5: USD/JPY dynamics - Our Macro Strategy team's fair value has fallen to ¥140 given narrowing rate differentials, while CFTC positioning looks still relatively long USD/JPY
![](images/c94aadc3fc6a88e12ae60f4b02b6a40504074026d0c0d98f1ee31daab18b58d8.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

![](images/b01e05bbbddfd7779495973d0597bb6ab8810c0967abb6fdc166949f7f8e718f.jpg)

## 4) Japan equities - Look for opportunities

Japan equities traded lower in an indiscriminate manner last Thursday and Friday, given the quadruple whammy outlined above. We may see further weakness in the coming days alongside USD/JPY downside and move more defensive in our Japan Focus List Changes . However, we already see significant value in parts of the market that benefit from the secular change in Japanese nominal GDP reflation and corporate reform (such as Financials, Real Estate and Capex Beneficiaries) and keep exposure to these themes.

Our economists expect the BoJ to keep policy rates negative in real terms, and our baseline for USD/JPY is a still-competitive ¥136 in mid-2025. Incoming results have continued to beat our analysts expectations with more expecting to lift their forecasts than to lower them. As such, we do not recommend investors move underweight Japan equities, unless a global recession becomes much more likely.

## Move defensive and waiting for soft landing signals

Stepping back to the regional Asia/EM equity view, weak seasonality and typically low summer liquidity are other factors that hold us back from recommending investors buy any near-term dips. August has historically been the worst performing calendar month (see below for last 29-year average), and it's not until November that seasonality turns favourable.

Exhibit 8: Average Monthly Price Return % over the past 29 Years (USD for MSCI EM and Local Currency for TOPIX) - Historically, August has been the worst month for both EM and Japan Equities
![](images/4f79f01653f3ffad073ee2d92fb72c77db366608149dc36c6619b0b6a4a7afdd.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research

Our Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator is not flagging any contrarian signals at this stage, and currently stands at the 52nd percentile. We typically recommend investors look to accumulate risk when the 4 week average for this indicator stands below the 15th percentile.

Exhibit 9: Morgan Stanley Asia Risk Sentiment Indicator vs. MSCI EM – as of August 2, 2024
![](images/49c302bd2c18f5ccbb3f3722a7f6606bb41aba26f6fc1e68f2ae19e3de86aa6e.jpg)
Source: FactSet. Bloomberg. EPFR. MSCI. Morgan Stanley Research. Note: Buy/Sell signals are generated if average weekly readings of the risk sentiment indicator in the last four weeks were below the 15th percentile or above the 85th percentile. The above red/green lines are tracking the forward 6M returns since the signal appeared. MSARSI methodology and its other details can be referred to in insight #1 in the QuantASIA launch report. Past performance is no guarantee of future results

Overall then, we recommend caution on Asia and EM equities in the coming weeks, and have already moved more defensive in our sector allocation and APxJ, GEM and Japan Focus Lists. We further increase our defensive positioning in our Japan Focus List Changes .

## Defensive characteristics of India and ASEAN coming to the fore

Post a soft ISM Manufacturing PMI print in the US and continuous rotation out of cyclical sectors, our call for turning defensive and reducing beta appears to be materializing. We hereby highlight the defensive nature of India and ASEAN markets, which have typically outperformed during EM bear markets and source revenue mainly domestically. In the prior EM bear markets, India, Malaysia, and Singapore have on average outperformed 6.9%, 8.1%, and 4.8%, respectively ( Exhibit 14 ).

Exhibit 14: Major Markets' Average USD Relative Returns vs. MSCI EM during EM Bear Markets since 1997 – Malaysia has proven to be defensive in prior EM bear markets
![](images/f9a05ed6eb5498f91c1b9e8d587ae9858a3d112ad7db91c2099688760486df8c.jpg)
Source: MSCI, Morgan Stanley Research

Malaysia and India's index-level beta against US and direct US revenue exposure are also among the lowest in APAC/EM, as shown in Exhibit 15 , making them a likely safe haven from negative spillovers from rest of the world.

Exhibit 15: Market Revenue Exposure to NA vs Equity Benchmark Index Beta to MSCI USA
![](images/950b25c3ed512a3bd9bef3ed0704c9be6060b2081d810102dfea42adefd944a3.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, Morgan Stanley Research; Data as of July 26, 2024; Note: Betas are calculated based on MSCI indices unless specified otherwise; weekly returns, rolling 52 weeks.

Our US Economists have called for three rate cuts by the Fed this year, starting from September, and we analyze historical market performance post the rate cuts. In the 12 months following the start of rate cutting cycle, Korea, Taiwan, India, Malaysia, Indonesia, and Thailand have outperformed MSCI EM ( Exhibit 16 ). Nonetheless, we note the changes of market structures and drivers over the years, for example the decline in foreign ownership of Rupiah-denominated bonds and significant differences in the stage of the tech cycles, and therefore do not solely rely on the historical analysis for predicting outperforming markets in the upcoming scenario.

Valuation-wise, all ASEAN markets are trading at below historical average 12mf P/E and thus not demanding. In our market allocation framework, we reiterate our OW stance on India and Singapore, while EW Philippines and Malaysia and UW Thailand and Indonesia, though we see increasing opportunities for ASEAN. Within markets we still focus on industry structure and company quality - see Asia EM Equity Strategy and Economics: Why It's Time to Take a Look at ASEAN - Launching Asean Best Business Models v1 (2 May 2024) for more details.

## Screening for Quality Defensives Without Momentum Risk

In line with our preference for defense and amid a potentially broader trend of profit taking, we screen our APxJ and Japan coverage for companies with high composite quality (MS MOST model), low 36 month beta, low 12 month price momentum, and OW/EW analyst rating.

## Japan Equity Strategy

## Sho Nakazawa

Prepare for further unwinding USD/JPY carry: As we discussed in "Hawkish Rate Hike: July MPM Review and Market Implications", Gov. Ueda's hawkish comments and the content of the policy statement point to risk of the next hike being brought forward, depending on upcoming data. We believe the main reason for TOPIX decline on 1-2 August was an unwind of crowded carry trades (long USD/JPY, long semis/equipment stocks, JPY yield curve steepener) following the combination of a hawkish BoJ (despite soft Japanese consumption) and a global risk-off move in response to weak US economic data.

Exhibit 21: TOPIX and Market implied pace of BoJ’s 25bp hikes in following 12 months: As markets have priced in sharper rate hiking cycle since Mid-July, TOPIX declined sharply
![](images/725999f7c24ac0f559556bcf44ee2a28254db68688b9a9f1ebab02cb8d6660e5.jpg)
Source: Bloomberg, FactSet, QUICK Workstation, Morgan Stanley Research

In short term, TOPIX could be volatile as further USD/JPY carry trade unwind might come: Our Macro Strategy team sees risk of a further decline in USD/JPY towards their estimated fair value of ¥140 as JPY carry positioning will be unwound further. In our "1Q results investment strategy playbook", we noted that investors will need to respond nimbly to FX moves given the differences in FX sensitivity across the market. In a period of correction for the currency pair, sector/stock picking based on FX sensitivity would likely become more effective.

FX sensitivity by sector is presented in Exhibit 22 . In Exhibit 23 and Exhibit 24 , we show names in our coverage with high and low FX sensitivity. Also, as we discussed in Global Exposure Guide 2024 – Japan, selecting stocks with high exposure to certain regions or countries is another tool to adjust stock portfolios in accordance with investors' view of relative growth.

The higher the mountain, the deeper the valley – buy into weakness: The main reason for the last few days' sharp correction is likely to have been popular carry trades (long USDJPY, long semis/equipment stocks, JPY yield curve steepener) being unwound on the back of a combination of a hawkish BoJ (despite soft fundamental background in Japan especially household consumption) and global risk-off move in response to weak US economic data.

In the short term, Japan equities could be volatile as we may see further USDJPY carry trade unwinding, and historically, August has been the worst month for Japan Equities as Exhibit 8 indicated.

However, fundamentally, our view on macro and micro structural changes in Japan is unchanged – As 1H result season gets closer, corporates may raise guidance and/or shareholder returns, and domestic consumption, which is stagnant right now, should gradually improve as real wage growth turns positive, which should positively affect investors’ conviction towards Japan.

![](images/106ee79272346dc04be2ecee8cd3817fb9b28ab41cd3a6d245f90d42ece2afef.jpg)
Note: Former TSE1 and Prime Market firms; total amount of share buybacks announced; based on officia announcements; as of 12 July. Source: Bloomberg, FactSet, QUICK Workstation, I-N Information Systems, Morgan Stanley Research

That said, earning risk may arise if further decline in USD/JPY towards implied fair value of ¥140 materializes: Corporate predictions for USD/JPY in the BOJ Tankan show that business plans are premised on USD/JPY in the low ¥140's range, which indicates there is still some buffer for corporates' profit outlooks despite the sharp JPY appreciation. Seasonal patterns of the analyst revisions suggest that analysts will revise their forecasts upward in August and September. However, we should be cautious about earning risk, as this analyst revision seasonality may not apply if a further fall in USD/JPY towards our strategists' fair value of ¥140 materializes.

## Japan Focus List Changes

We add Fast Retailing (9983.T), NTT (9432.T), and Kansai Electric Power (9503.T) to our Japan Focus List, while removing Renesas Electoronics (6723.T), UT Group (2146.T), and Isetan Mitsukoshi Holdings (3099.T). For Fast Retailing, we see higher pricing power sustaining Japan’s reflation journey, while NTT and Kansai Electric Power are domestic/ defensive exposures that should provide a relative safe harbour in futrther volatility. We also see the prospect of buying support from individual investors into NTT's high dividend yield, while Yen appreciation is a tailwind for Kansai Electric Power.

Source: Morgan Stanley Research

Fast Retailing (9983.T): F8/24 3Q OP was ¥144.7bn, up YoY and ahead of the IFIS consensus. Greater China, which had been a concern, saw a steep decline in profit and did not provide confirmation of an earnings inflection, but the UNIQLO business in North America, Europe, Southeast Asia and Japan performed well. Our analyst sees the continued strong performance of the North American and European UNIQLO businesses and the confirmation of improved profitability for domestic UNIQLO business as positives. The unit price per item has been rising since the weighting of products with higher unit prices (bottoms, knitwear, bra tops, etc.) has increased. Significant improvement in domestic profitability is expected to be a driver of earnings growth.

NTT (9432.T): NTT is the company with the largest increase in individual shareholders in 2023, driven by the launch of a new small investment tax-exempt system (NISA). The recent 3Q results showed fundamentals are challenging, with the company guiding for a 6% YoY decline in OP which is below the market forecast, but there is the prospect of buying support from individual investors in a bear market. With regard to shareholder return, NTT plans to increase its dividend by 2% YoY.

Kansai Electric Power (9503.T): Recurring profit in F3/25 1Q was ¥169bn (-37.0% YoY), falling but broadly in line with the consensus expectation. This reached 46.9% of the fullyear plan of ¥360bn, exceeding the 5-year average 1Q achievement rate of 28.6%, marking strong progress. The operation of nuclear power plants is expected to increase relative price competitiveness in electricity sales. Our analyst believes the company’s relatively high ROE within the industry is not fully priced in, and sees the shares as undervalued. If the yen appreciates in the future due to the shrinking of yen carry positions, a decrease in the cost of fuel procured from overseas may support earnings.

## Fund Flows and Positioning

## Kristal Ji

• Aggregate flows (EPFR, to July 31, 2024): EM recorded inflows, particularly into China, Taiwan and South Korea. China inflows originated from domestic China funds, while active long-only US and EU funds and offshore China funds continued selling Chinese equities ( Exhibit 40 ).

• Flows by market (EPFR, to July 31, 2024): Relative to AUM, China, Taiwan and South Korea recorded the largest inflows within EM, while Mexico, Brazil and Indonesia recorded the largest outflows. ( Exhibit 34 ).

• Japan flows (JPX, to July 26, 2024): Japan foreign investors net sold US \$9.9bn in the week ended July 26, 2024, bringing YTD net bought down to US\$1.5bn, while retail investors saw inflows of US\$5.0bn, bringing YTD net bought to US\$4.5bn ( Exhibit 37 and Exhibit 38 ).

• Asia/EM Positioning (EPFR, to June 2024): Underweights on India in GEM fund allocations stand at 81bps as managers have slipped behind its rising index weight. China stands at 67bps underweight vs. benchmark. Global long-only funds remained underweight Japan by 52bps as of end-June (see Exhibit 35 and Exhibit 31 ).

## STOCK RATINGS

Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell. Investors should carefully read the definitions of all ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone. In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice. An investor's decision to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

## Global Stock Ratings Distribution

(as of July 31, 2024)

The Stock Ratings described below apply to Morgan Stanley's Fundamental Equity Research and do not apply to Debt Research produced by the Firm.

For disclosure purposes only (in accordance with FINRA requirements), we include the category headings of Buy, Hold, and Sell alongside our ratings of Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight. Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy, Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold, and sell but represent recommended relative weightings (see definitions below). ## Analyst Stock Ratings

Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months

Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.

Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months

## Analyst Industry Views

Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below. Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.
